      28 +                                                               
      29 +- **Enums vs. free text vs. objects.** Closed vocabularies the 
         +analysis groups on are **enums** — `TreatmentOutcome`, `ReasonP
         +rescribed`, `ReasonNotTaken`, `ComorbidCondition`, `PathwayStep
         +` — so `value_counts`/joins get stable categories and membershi
         +p is checked at the model boundary (`enforce_validation`). Open
         +, high-cardinality, not-yet-canonical fields stay **free text**
         +: `biologic_type`, `TreatmentRecord.name`/`treatment_class` (a 
         +branded-drug + treatment-class registry is future work — `docs/
         +TODO.md`). Repeated, multi-attribute things are **structured ob
         +jects/lists**: `treatment_records` (a list of `TreatmentRecord`
         +, each carrying `before_biologic`, the flag that answers Q3), `
         +demographics`, and `referral_pathway` (`list[PathwayStep]`). Th
         +e visible cost of the free-text choice shows up in the **Q3 tab
         +le** — `conventional_therapy`/`conventional` and case variants 
         +are one class left un-normalised.                              
      30 +- **Three states, not one null.** "Not mentioned" (absence), "e
         +xplicitly denied" (negation) and "cut off" (truncation) are kep
         +t as *separate* signals rather than collapsed into a null: abse
         +nce → `biologic_not_mentioned` + null/empty fields + `NOT_MENTI
         +ONED`; negation → the `biologic_prescribed` vs. `biologic_taken
         +` pair + a specific `ReasonNotTaken` (`EXPLICIT_DENIAL`/`INSURA
         +NCE_PROBLEMS`/…); truncation → `churn` + `JOURNEY_CUT_OFF`. A `
         +NOT_APPLICABLE` member marks "genuinely doesn't apply" so it is
         +n't confused with a blank "not yet annotated" cell. This is the
         + split the churn table under Q4 operationalises.               
      31 +- **Evidence / auditability.** `evidence_notes` exists but is *
         +*deferred** — per-field snippets cost tokens and prompt complex
         +ity. The audit trail today is the **logged raw model output** (
         +`logs/extract.log` keeps the system prompt, schema, input and p
         +re-validation response per call) plus the **gold-set comparison
         +** (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`). Per-field evidence/confidence is stre
         +tch task 2.

  ⎿  Added 6 lines
      37  - **Prompt design for uncertainty.** How do you instruct the mo
          del to separate absence, negation, and truncation?
      38  - **Determinism and reproducibility.** Temperature, structured 
          output mode, seed, caching — what did you pick and why?
      39  
      40 +**Answers (provisional).**                                     
      41 +                                                               
      42 +- **Single-shot, batched — not multi-stage.** One system prompt
         + extracts *all* `PatientLabels` fields in a single structured c
         +all per patient; transcripts are then **batched** (10 per reque
         +st) only to fit Gemini's free-tier ~20-requests/day cap — batch
         +ing ≠ staging, it's still one pass over every field, just sever
         +al patients per call. Single-shot minimises calls (decisive und
         +er the rate cap), keeps the code simple, and lets the model set
         + inter-dependent fields (`biologic_prescribed`/`biologic_taken`
         +, `churn`, `referral_pathway`) with the whole story in view. Th
         +e cost: every field competes for attention in one prompt, so th
         +e hard fields suffer — the EDA shows `churn` unreliable and the
         + GP node under-emitted. A multi-stage split (per-section prompt
         +s run concurrently, with inspectable intermediates) is the #1 i
         +tem in *Next Steps* below; it trades more calls + orchestration
         + for better per-field accuracy.                                
      43 +- **Uncertainty: absence vs. negation vs. truncation.** The pro
         +mpt separates them by construction, onto the three schema mecha
         +nisms above. A global rule — *"Leave a field null or empty when
         + the transcript does not support a confident value"* — covers *
         +*absence**. **Negation** rides on the prescribed-vs-taken boole
         +ans + the `ReasonNotTaken` enum. **Truncation** gets the most e
         +xplicit instruction: judge `churn` from how the transcript *end
         +s*, treat a trailing "…" as a cut-off *only* if "…" isn't a rec
         +urring stylistic device, prefer `false` when ambiguous, and — c
         +ritically — *"keep this truncation signal distinct from a topic
         + simply being absent … (that is 'not mentioned', not churn)."* 
         +Honest limit: truncation is a lexical/structural property of th
         +e transcript's end that the narrative under-determines, so the 
         +instruction alone isn't enough — the gold audit caught a false 
         +positive (P016) and a false negative (P049), and a deterministi
         +c tail-of-text rule would do better (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`).     
      44 +- **Determinism & reproducibility.** Three levers, in decreasin
         +g order of leverage. **(1) Structured-output mode** — `response
         +_format={"type": "json_object", "response_schema": PatientLabel
         +s.model_json_schema(), "enforce_validation": true}`: a Pydantic
         +-derived schema constrains the *shape* and enum membership, and
         + LiteLLM validates the reply (raising `JSONSchemaValidationErro
         +r` on a mismatch), so format/parse variance is removed and down
         +stream pandas never meets an off-vocabulary value. **(2) Persis
         +ted predictions** — each chunk writes one validated JSON per pa
         +tient to `data/out/`, and the whole analysis (this README) read
         +s *only* those frozen artefacts with no API call, so every numb
         +er and plot is **exactly reproducible run-to-run** regardless o
         +f LLM nondeterminism; this is the real guarantee. **(3) Decodin
         +g params** — `temperature` is left at the provider default this
         + iteration; pinning it to `0` (greedy) and adding a seed where 
         +Gemini honours it is the open determinism step (`docs/TODO.md`)
         +, though with a reasoning-capable model bit-exact output isn't 
         +guaranteed anyway, and (1)+(2) already remove the variance that
         + affects the answers. No explicit prompt **caching** yet — the 
         +static system prompt + schema are identical across calls, so im
         +plicit context caching may apply, and cached-token counts are l
         +ogged (`docs/TELEMETRY.md`).                                   
      45 +

⏺ Found the pytest bug — and it's a textbook post-mv venv breakage. The
  diagnostic is conclusive, but let me confirm the exact mechanism and also

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Both hypotheses confirmed — and the diagnosis is more interesting than
  "missing dependency":

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The pytest failure is a stale-venv shebang, not a missing package.
  .venv/bin/pytest is a Python console-script whose shebang still hardcodes the
  old path: #!/Users/shoutt/projects/mama_health/chronicity/.venv/bin/python3.
  After mv chronicity crohnicity that interpreter path no longer exists, so the
  OS can't spawn it → Failed to spawn: pytest: No such file or directory. ruff
  and ty kept working because they're Mach-O native binaries (no shebang),
  immune to the path change — which is exactly why "other SETUP.md steps
  worked."

  Why uv sync didn't fix it: the package was already present, so plain uv sync
  left the stale console-script in place — it doesn't rewrite shebangs of
  already-installed entry points. evidence_notes is also confirmed: 50/50 
  predictions populate it with a model-written rationale, so my README line was
  wrong.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Let me apply the real fix (force a reinstall so the shebang is rewritten to
  the new path) and verify:

  finding for you: plain uv sync was insufficient — you needed uv sync

      440 +### Rebuild the answers doc

      88  
      89 +**Why `--reinstall` and not plain `uv sync`:** a rename leaves 
         +the venv's Python console-scripts                              
      90 +(`.venv/bin/pytest`, …) with a shebang hardcoding the *old* int
         +erpreter path, so `uv run pytest`                              
      91 +fails with `Failed to spawn: pytest — No such file or directory
         + (os error 2)`. Plain `uv sync` sees                           
      92 +the packages already installed and won't regenerate the scripts
         +; `--reinstall` forces the                                     
      93 +entry-point shebangs to be rewritten to the new path. Native bi
         +naries (`ruff`, `ty`) have no shebang                          
      94 +and keep working — which is why *only* `pytest` breaks after a 
         +rename. (If `--reinstall` ever isn't                           
      95 +enough, `rm -rf .venv && uv sync` recreates the venv from scrat
         +ch.)                                                           
      96 +

          claude.com/docs/en/output-styles) to "Explanatory" mode using `
          /config`. This mode "Provides educational “Insights” in between
           helping you complete software engineering tasks. Helps you und
          erstand implementation choices and codebase patterns." Rather t
          han CC writing the code, I copied out the code snippets by hand
          , so I could understand the implications of each step.

⏺ Recorded. Now the direct answer to your architecture question — yes, all of it
  makes sense, and your two refinements about the static input are exactly
  right:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Dagster as the day-of entry point is the standard "orchestrator over a 
  library" shape — with one rule that keeps it clean: library-first. Both
  dagster dev and src/main.py should import the same src/ functions; an asset
  wraps a function, it must not shell out to uv run python src/main.py (a
  subprocess loses typed data-passing and per-patient failure visibility — the
  very thing the UI is for). So the CLI and the gate aren't "inside" Dagster;
  they're alternate frontends/checks over the same units.

  Static input ⇒ no sensor, and that's correct twice over. A sensor exists to
  detect external state changes; data/in/interviews.json is static and
  committed, so a sensor would never fire. A schedule is also wrong here —
  re-running identical input on a cron just burns calls against the
  20-requests/day cap for an identical result. So the right trigger is manual / 
  on-demand (materialise in the UI). A sensor only earns its place when the
  input goes dynamic — new transcripts via the FastAPI endpoint — which is
  precisely why that lives in the README's Next Steps while Dagster is an MVP
  TODO.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  The tests/.sh gate sits on a different axis from the pipeline: it verifies, it
  doesn't produce artefacts. So it's either kept as the standalone quality
  gate, or (cleaner, later) expressed as Dagster asset checks (@dg.asset_check)
  so a failed schema-validation or render lights up red on the relevant asset.
  Treating pytest as a pipeline "sub-call" would be a category error — I framed
  it that way in the TODO note.

⏺ Recorded — the gate is now committed as a Dagster asset check in the
  Orchestration decision note, with the four sub-checks named explicitly (ruff
  lint, ruff format, ty typing, pytest unit tests) plus the render smoke test.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Why @dg.asset_check is the right Dagster primitive here (not another asset):
  an asset produces a materialised artefact in the lineage graph; a check
  asserts a pass/fail condition and renders as a green/red badge attached to an
  asset without polluting the lineage. Your gate doesn't produce data — it
  judges code health — so modelling it as a check keeps the graph honest
  (interviews → predictions → … → eda) while still surfacing a lint/type/test
  failure "at a high level in the UI," which was your whole goal. The render
  smoke test naturally hangs off the eda/report asset; the lint/type/unit-test
  trio can attach to a root asset (or a tiny repo asset) since they're
  repo-wide.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  That closes out the design decisions. The two concrete build tasks now queued
  in TODO have a natural dependency order — the src/post_extraction_eda.py 
  refactor (and the broader notebooks→src/ move) makes every stage an importable
  function, which is the prerequisite that lets the Dagster pipeline compose
  them as assets + checks.

  My recommendation: start with the post_extraction_eda.py refactor (you asked
  for it explicitly, it's API-free since it only reads data/out/, and it
  unblocks Dagster). Want me to proceed with that — extracting the README.qmd
  chunks into typed functions, repointing the .qmd cells to import them, and
  adding pytest coverage so it passes the gate?

          "gemini/...", ...)`, not the raw `google-genai` SDK. The SDK is
           only present because it was used in the notebook to sanity-che
          ck the API key before LiteLLM was wired in. LiteLLM gives us `r
          esponse_format={"type": "json_object"}` and a `response_schema`
           hook for Gemini (with optional `enforce_validation: true`) — p
          refer that over hand-parsing model output.
      61  - **Reproducibility**: `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` supports reasoni
          ng (`litellm.supports_reasoning(...)` returns True) and caching
          /TTL. If you add prompt caching or thinking-mode toggles, docum
          ent them where they're set — they affect token accounting and r
          un-to-run determinism.

          son`), `data/out/` is for generated artefacts, `data/prompts/`

❯ let's decide if this should be orchestrated with dagster, i think so 

⏺ The format is simple (:::module pulls docstrings at build time). I'll
  consolidate into two complete pages — api/src.md (all 7 source modules) and
  api/tests.md (all test modules) — replacing the two single-module pages, so
  the docsite covers the whole codebase and stays dynamic. And I'll answer your
  Dagster question by capturing the decision.

  On "should the docsite build be orchestrated with Dagster?" — yes, with one 
  important nuance:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The docsite is a code-dependent asset, not a data-dependent one. The README
  render and referral graphs read data/out/ → they're genuinely downstream of
  predictions. But mkdocs build reads docstrings from source, with no dependency
  on the predictions at all. So in the Dagster DAG it should be an independent 
  final asset (no upstream data edge), bundled into the same dagster dev run so
  one command rebuilds every artifact — but wired honestly so the graph doesn't
  imply the docsite depends on the data. Modelling it downstream of predictions
  would be a false dependency.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

⏺ Committed and pushed — clean. Now to finalise Dagster. Reviewing the current
  pipeline.py (2 assets, per-patient extract = 50 calls) against all the
  decisions we captured:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  What "correctly specified" means here, from our prior decisions: (1)
  extraction must be chunked (run_chunked, 5 calls) — the per-patient version
  blows the 20/day cap; (2) readme and referral_graphs are data-downstream of
  predictions (they read data/out/), wired with deps=[...] since they write
  files rather than pass values; (3) docsite is independent (documents source
  docstrings, no data edge) — modelling it downstream would be a false
  dependency; (4) static input ⇒ no sensor. The README-render asset is the
  post-extraction-EDA artefact (quarto runs the post_extraction_eda functions),
  so no separate EDA asset is needed — that keeps it minimal.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  deliberately brief:

⏺ Two issues surfaced — both fixable, and exactly why we validate before handing
  it to you:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Dagster's -f loader doesn't put src/ on sys.path — so the bare sibling imports
  (config, main, referral_pathway_analysis…) that work under pytest
  (pythonpath=[src]) and ty (src as root) fail when Dagster loads the file
  directly. The fix mirrors what README.qmd already does: self-bootstrap src/
  onto the path at the top of the file, so the documented dagster dev -f 
  src/pipeline.py "just works." (Also: pipeline.py needed ruff format.)
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

⏺ Launching the Dagster run — two canonical ways (verified)

  Both interfaces run the same asset definitions; they differ only in how you
  trigger them:

  1. From the UI (canonical for dagster dev) — the dev server you have running
  is the launcher. In the browser (http://127.0.0.1:3000): Assets tab →
  "Materialize all" (or select predictions/readme/… → Materialize). The server
  stays running in its terminal; you click in the browser. This is the standard
  interactive dev loop.
  2. Headless from a second terminal (CLI) — no UI needed, runs in its own
  process: uv run dagster asset materialize --select "*" -f src/pipeline.py.
  Good for scripted/CI runs. (Note -f scopes to that one file; -m/--package-name
  would load a module/package.)

  So: not the same terminal as dagster dev (it's blocked serving) — either click
  in the UI, or use a second terminal with dagster asset materialize. For your
  50-transcript E2E, "Materialize all" in the UI is simplest and gives you the
  per-stage graph view.

  Sources:
  - Different ways to materialize assets in Dagster — skytowner
  - Materialize a partition via dagster asset materialize CLI — dagster 
  discussion #18114
  - assets API — Dagster Docs

  Now your "good catch" follow-up — adding the biologic note to the plot and the
  README (as an error-handling/limitation note), plus documenting the headless
  run command in Usage:

⏺ Good observation — and it reveals a real distinction worth documenting. Here's
  what's happening:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  .env and config.json are loaded by different layers, which is why only one 
  shows in the log. Dagster itself auto-discovers and loads .env (secrets like
  GEMINI_API_KEY) and logs it. config.json is loaded by our src/config.py
  (json.loads(...)) at import time — which fires when Dagster imports
  pipeline.py (at dagster dev startup / code load), not per-materialize. It's
  silent because it's a plain json.loads, not a Dagster mechanism. So:
  config.json (model, paths, chunk_size) is read once at load and its values
  drive the predictions asset during materialization — exactly as you'd expect,
  just without a log line.

  Two other things that log surfaced: Using temporary directory … DAGSTER_HOME
  means run history isn't persisted (the temp dir is wiped on exit) — set
  DAGSTER_HOME to keep it. And the dg dev supersession warning is cosmetic;
  dagster dev still works.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Let me document this in DEV_SETUP and fold DAGSTER_HOME + config-visibility
  into the logging TODO:

❯ explain this:  ▎ The reappearing Pylance import "dagster" could not be resolved is the same IDE false positive (VS Code using the stale …/azure_devops/…/.venv); ty and the gate resolve it fine. Pointing the VS Code interpreter at .venv/bin/python clears it for good. 
⏺ Two things. First, the Pylance explanation you asked for:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Pylance ≠ ty. Pylance is VS Code's built-in Python language server
  (Microsoft's), and it resolves imports against whatever interpreter VS Code is
  pointed at. Your shell exports VIRTUAL_ENV=…/azure_devops/…/adhoc/.venv (a
  different project's env that's "active"), and VS Code is using that
  interpreter — which has no dagster installed → "could not be resolved." But
  that env is not this project's. The project env is ./.venv (where uv add 
  dagster-webserver installed it), and that's what uv run …, ty check, and the
  gate all use — so they resolve dagster fine. Hence: real dependency, wrong
  interpreter → false positive. It re-fires every time you touch pipeline.py
  because Pylance re-analyses with the same wrong interpreter. Fix: VS Code
  Command Palette → "Python: Select Interpreter" → ./.venv/bin/python (or unset 
  VIRTUAL_ENV before launching VS Code).
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Now run-history persistence. I'll point DAGSTER_HOME at logs/dagster/ (so run
  history + Dagster's text logs persist alongside extract.log), with a
  dagster.yaml that routes Dagster's run logs to a flat logs/dagster/runs.log.

❯ explain the .gitkeep for dagster 

⏺ Good question to probe — and the honest answer is that .gitkeep is redundant 
  here:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  .gitkeep is a convention, not a git feature. Git tracks files, never empty
  directories — so an empty logs/dagster/ would simply not exist after a clone.
  The community trick is to commit a placeholder file (conventionally named
  .gitkeep, though any filename works) purely to force git to track the
  directory. I added it so DAGSTER_HOME=logs/dagster would have a folder present
  after clone, since I git-ignore the generated run-storage.

  But it's unnecessary in this case: I also commit logs/dagster/dagster.yaml
  (un-ignored via !logs/dagster/dagster.yaml), and that committed file already 
  keeps the directory tracked. .gitkeep only earns its place when a directory
  would otherwise be entirely empty. So I'll remove it.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

✻ Cooked for 7m 57s
❯ logs/dagster/dagster.yaml seems a strange buried folder for a .yml settings   
  file. is this canonical for dagster/python repos?                             

⏺ Fair instinct — let me verify the canonical pattern rather than assert it:

⏺ Web Search("dagster.yaml location DAGSTER_HOME instance config where does
            dagster.yaml live canonical")
  ⎿  Did 1 search in 6s

⏺ Verified — and your instinct is right: it's not the canonical convention.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  dagster.yaml is the instance config, and Dagster only reads it from 
  $DAGSTER_HOME/dagster.yaml — its location is defined by DAGSTER_HOME, so you
  can't move the file without moving DAGSTER_HOME. The canonical local
  DAGSTER_HOME is a dedicated directory (the docs use ~/.dagster_home), not a
  folder nested inside logs/. So logs/dagster/dagster.yaml is valid (it
  genuinely is $DAGSTER_HOME/dagster.yaml when DAGSTER_HOME=logs/dagster) but
  unconventional. It looks buried because I pointed DAGSTER_HOME into logs/ to
  honour "run history in logs/" — which conflated two different things:
  Dagster's internal instance state (a sqlite DB — not really a "log") and the
  readable run log (the text record you actually want in logs/).
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Cleaner, more canonical split that still meets your goal:
  - DAGSTER_HOME=.dagster_home/ (repo-root, git-ignored) — the conventional
  dedicated instance home; holds dagster.yaml + the sqlite storage (regenerated
  internal state, not audit material).
  - dagster.yaml routes the readable run log → logs/dagster.log — the text
  record, in your canonical logs/ alongside extract.log.

  So the config sits in its conventional instance home, and the audit log lands
  in logs/. The trade-off: Dagster's sqlite state lives in .dagster_home/
  (git-ignored, not committed) — correct, since it's internal state, while
  logs/dagster.log is the human-readable record.

  Want me to restructure to that? It's contained: move dagster.yaml →
  .dagster_home/, repoint the documented DAGSTER_HOME, route the log to
  logs/dagster.log, update .gitignore. Or keep the current self-contained
  logs/dagster/ if you'd rather have everything in one place — your call on repo
  layout.

  Sources:
  - Instance configuration — Dagster Docs
  - dagster.yaml reference — Dagster Docs
  - Running Dagster locally — Dagster Docs

❯ why git ignore the .yaml given we need to show our working and there are no secrets there? 

❯ i think we should show it to show pipeline 

⏺ First — to your question: the .yaml is not git-ignored. Look at the rule:

  .dagster_home/*            ← ignore everything in the instance home...
  !.dagster_home/dagster.yaml ← ...except the config, which IS committed

  The ! negation re-includes it, and the git check-ignore test just confirmed
  it: only .dagster_home/history (the regenerated sqlite storage) is ignored —
  dagster.yaml is tracked and committed, exactly so it shows the pipeline
  wiring. Plus pipeline.py itself (the orchestration) is committed in src/ and
  rendered in the docsite. So the working is fully shown; only the binary
  run-storage is excluded. I'll make that explicit in the docs.

⏺ "Launch a run" vs "materialize an asset" (per the docs)

  They're hierarchical, not alternatives:
  - Materialize an asset = execute that asset's function and persist its result
  (emits an AssetMaterialization event). It's the asset-centric action —
  "produce/refresh this data object."
  - Launch a run = start an execution — a run is the container that tracks one
  execution (its own run ID, status, logs, "Runs" tab entry).
  - The link: a materialization always happens inside a run. Clicking
  "Materialize all" launches a run whose job is to materialize the selected
  assets. So for your pipeline, "Materialize all" = one run that materializes
  interviews → predictions → readme/referral_graphs + docsite.

  Sources: Assets — Dagster Docs · execution API — Dagster Docs · Ways to 
  materialize assets

  Setup check — one thing to fix before the run ⚠️

  The server's up and the DAG loads, so the run itself will work. But 
  DAGSTER_HOME is still not set — the log says it plainly:

  Using temporary directory /…/.tmp_dagster_home_na05hsvr for storage.
  This will be removed when dagster dev exits.
  … set the environment variable DAGSTER_HOME …

  And the .env line confirms it: Loaded environment variables from .env file: 
  GEMINI_API_KEY, RUN_LIVE_TESTS — no DAGSTER_HOME. So if you materialize now,
  the run works but its history + logs/dagster.log get wiped on exit — no
  persisted record.

  Fix before running (then restart dagster dev):
  printf '\nDAGSTER_HOME=%s/.dagster_home\n' "$PWD" >> .env   # run from repo 
  root
  Re-launch uv run dagster dev -f src/pipeline.py; you should then see it load
  DAGSTER_HOME (not "Using temporary directory"), and after Materialize all,
  logs/dagster.log will appear.

  Otherwise the setup is good: Materialize all → 50 transcripts via the chunked
  5 calls (within the 20/day cap) → EDA/README + graphs + docsite. (The dg dev
  supersession line is cosmetic — ignore it.)

  Want me to append that .env line for you, or will you add it? Once it
  persists, the E2E run is your call to make.

❯ good test of centralised config file 

⏺ DAGSTER_HOME is loaded now (the log shows it in .env and there's no "temporary
  directory" line) — persistence + logs/dagster.log are live.

  But your hypothesis — "should just impact config.json?" — is the instructive 
  part:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Centralised config wins for value changes, not structural ones. If you'd just
  moved data/out somewhere else (one dir → one dir), it'd be a single
  config.json edit and every OUT_DIR user would follow automatically — that's
  the payoff you're testing. But this is a structural split: one dir became two
  (json/ + html/), introducing a distinction the code didn't have before. So the
  modules must now choose the right constant — predictions → JSON_DIR, graphs
  read JSON_DIR + write HTML_DIR. config.json holds the values (in one place ✓);
  the code still has to reference the new names. So: config.json + the ~6
  modules that touch those paths.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  So config alone isn't enough here — let me finish the module rewiring
  (predictions/graphs/eda/tests → the new constants):

⏺ Good catch on the JSON-in-both-dirs point — let me clarify the structure,
  because the data/in vs data/out prefix is what disambiguates it:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The semantic comes from the in/out prefix, not the leaf name.
  data/in/interviews.json = the pre-extraction input; data/out/json/P*.json =
  the post-extraction predictions. Same format (JSON), opposite role — and the
  in/out parent already encodes the role, so data/out/json reads unambiguously
  as "the JSON the pipeline produced." It's named by format (matching html/,
  plots/), which is why I kept your json/html choice.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  If you'd rather name by content (data/out/predictions/ + data/out/graphs/),
  that's now a near-config-only change (the 2 values in config.json + renaming
  the dirs) — a nice payoff of the centralisation. Say the word; for now I'll
  finish with your json/html. Let me wire the last module (the graph generator,
  which reads json/ and writes html/) + the doc references:

⏺ The doc confirms the key point for your compatibility question:

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  [tool.dg] and dagster.yaml are orthogonal layers — fully compatible. [tool.dg]
  (in pyproject.toml) is project / code-location discovery — "where do the
  Definitions live" (code_location_target_module). dagster.yaml in $DAGSTER_HOME
  (.dagster_home/) is instance config — storage + log routing. dg dev uses
  both: [tool.dg] to find our pipeline.defs, dagster.yaml for where runs/logs
  persist. So our existing .dagster_home setup stays exactly as-is; we just add
  the project config.

❯ so dg check defs would be run from our .sh for dev setup tools? or is this redundant? 

⏺ ✅ dg fully wired — gate green, render clean, and dg loads all 5 assets
  (interviews, predictions, referral_graphs, readme, docsite).

  On your dg check defs-in-the-gate question — no, it'd be wrong to add it (and 
  would break the gate):

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  dg check defs validates the Components layout, which we don't use. It looks
  for a src/pipeline/defs/ folder and errors ("Defs folder not found") because
  we use a plain Definitions object via code_location_target_module. So in the
  .sh it would fail every time — redundant and inapplicable. The command that
  actually validates our defs load is dagster definitions validate -f 
  src/pipeline.py (or dg list defs).
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  And is adding that (a defs-load check) to the gate worth it? Mostly redundant:
  - ty check src tests already statically catches the common breakage — import
  errors, bad types, undefined names in pipeline.py.
  - A defs-load check would add only marginal coverage (e.g. a malformed asset
  graph that imports fine), at the cost of making the lean, env-light gate
  heavier (it must load the code location).
  - We've been running dagster definitions validate / dg list defs manually 
  after every pipeline.py edit — which is the right cadence for a 5-asset DAG.

  Recommendation: keep the gate as ruff + ruff format + ty + pytest (fast,
  hermetic); leave defs-load validation as the manual post-edit step. I can add
  it to the gate as an optional step if you'd prefer belt-and-braces, but for
  the MVP it's not pulling its weight.

  dg is ready. Per your plan: run the E2E (uv run dg dev -d src → Materialize 
  all, or uv run dg launch --assets "*" -d src), check the artefacts in
  data/out/{json,html} + logs/dagster.log, then commit — and I'll do the
  TODO-vs-(README + TASK_INSTRUCTIONS) question-coverage review. Want me to
  start that review now while you run the pipeline?

⏺ dg dev -d src failed because -d alone isn't a valid loader — you must also
  tell it what to load (-m/-f/-w). The good news: dg dev supports -f
  (--python-file), exactly like dagster dev -f. Since pipeline.py already
  self-bootstraps src onto the path, dg dev -f src/pipeline.py should be the
  simplest working form. Let me confirm via the non-blocking list (and check
  launch's flags):

  Rather than guess, let me definitively test the two candidate forms with a
  short timed launch (separate ports, auto-killed):

❯ i can test it 

⏺ dg list defs rejects -m/-f (those loaders live only on dev/launch; list is
  project-mode, hence cd src && dg list defs). So I can't verify the dev loader
  non-blockingly — and you're at the terminal, so you test it. Two valid forms
  to try (both pair a loader with --working-directory, which is the missing
  piece):

  # 1 — module + working-directory (your --working-directory lead; from repo 
  root so .env loads)
  uv run dg dev -m pipeline -d src

  # 2 — file loader (direct analog of the proven `dagster dev -f`; pipeline.py 
  self-bootstraps src)
  uv run dg dev -f src/pipeline.py

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Why these work where -d src alone didn't: dg dev needs to be told what to load
  — a code-location target (-m module, -f file, or -w workspace).
  -d/--working-directory only sets where Python imports from; it's not a target
  by itself, hence "Invalid set of CLI arguments." Form 1 says "load module
  pipeline, importing from src/"; form 2 says "load this file" (and the file
  fixes its own path). Both run from repo root so .env (DAGSTER_HOME,
  GEMINI_API_KEY) still loads.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Heads-up: the docs currently show the broken dg dev -d src (from before we
  found it needs a loader). Tell me which form works (or paste the output) and
  I'll fix pipeline.py, README Usage, and DEV_SETUP to the confirmed command in
  one pass — rather than commit another guess.

❯ launch a run or materialize an asset in this dg workflow? for the full 50-set 

⏺ For the full 50-set, you materialize all the assets — which is launching one 
  run. They're not a choice; "materialize all" is the asset-centric framing of
  the same action that "launch a run" describes:

  - UI (dg dev -m pipeline -d src → http://127.0.0.1:3000): Assets → 
  "Materialize all". One run executes the whole DAG — interviews → predictions →
  {readme, referral_graphs} + docsite.
  - Headless CLI: uv run dg launch --assets "*" -m pipeline -d src — dg launch
  is "launch a run"; --assets "*" selects all assets to materialize in it.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  For your pipeline, "Materialize all" = the full 50-transcript run. It's a
  single run; materialising predictions triggers the chunked extraction (5 
  Gemini calls, the only API spend), then readme/referral_graphs materialise
  downstream and docsite independently. Don't materialise assets one-by-one —
  that's more runs (and readme/graphs would run on stale predictions). One
  "Materialize all" does the whole DAG in dependency order.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  Root cause: non-deterministic extraction + hardcoded run-specifics. We never
  pinned temperature=0, so a re-run reshuffles the long-tail details (which
  patients have a null reason, which biologics get mislabelled) even while the
  headline answers (Q1 %, gold F1) stay put. Hardcoding patient IDs in prose is
  therefore fragile — the dynamic tables are the real source of truth; the prose
  should describe patterns, not name shifting cases.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  reference gp_count dynamically instead of "9/50".

⏺ Update(README.qmd)
  ⎿  Added 1 line, removed 1 line
      41  
      42  - **Single-shot, batched — not multi-stage.** One system prompt
           extracts *all* `PatientLabels` fields in a single structured c
          all per patient; transcripts are then **batched** (10 per reque
          st) only to fit Gemini's free-tier ~20-requests/day cap — batch
          ing ≠ staging, it's still one pass over every field, just sever
          al patients per call. Single-shot minimises calls (decisive und
          er the rate cap), keeps the code simple, and lets the model set
           inter-dependent fields (`biologic_prescribed`/`biologic_taken`
          , `churn`, `referral_pathway`) with the whole story in view. Th
          e cost: every field competes for attention in one prompt, so th
          e hard fields suffer — the EDA shows `churn` unreliable and the
           GP node under-emitted. A multi-stage split (per-section prompt
          s run concurrently, with inspectable intermediates) is the #1 i
          tem in *Next Steps* below; it trades more calls + orchestration
           for better per-field accuracy.
      43  - **Uncertainty: absence vs. negation vs. truncation.** The pro
          mpt separates them by construction, onto the three schema mecha
          nisms above. A global rule — *"Leave a field null or empty when
           the transcript does not support a confident value"* — covers *
          *absence**. **Negation** rides on the prescribed-vs-taken boole
          ans + the `ReasonNotTaken` enum. **Truncation** gets the most e
          xplicit instruction: judge `churn` from how the transcript *end
          s*, treat a trailing "…" as a cut-off *only* if "…" isn't a rec
          urring stylistic device, prefer `false` when ambiguous, and — c
          ritically — *"keep this truncation signal distinct from a topic
           simply being absent … (that is 'not mentioned', not churn)."* 
          Honest limit: truncation is a lexical/structural property of th
          e transcript's end that the narrative under-determines, so the 
          instruction alone isn't enough — the gold audit caught a false 
          positive (P016) and a false negative (P049), and a deterministi
          c tail-of-text rule would do better (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`).
      44 -- **Determinism & reproducibility.** Three levers, in decreasin
         -g order of leverage. **(1) Structured-output mode** — `response
         -_format={"type": "json_object", "response_schema": PatientLabel
         -s.model_json_schema(), "enforce_validation": true}`: a Pydantic
         --derived schema constrains the *shape* and enum membership, and
         - LiteLLM validates the reply (raising `JSONSchemaValidationErro
         -r` on a mismatch), so format/parse variance is removed and down
         -stream pandas never meets an off-vocabulary value. **(2) Persis
         -ted predictions** — each chunk writes one validated JSON per pa
         -tient to `data/out/`, and the whole analysis (this README) read
         -s *only* those frozen artefacts with no API call, so every numb
         -er and plot is **exactly reproducible run-to-run** regardless o
         -f LLM nondeterminism; this is the real guarantee. **(3) Decodin
         -g params** — `temperature` is left at the provider default this
         - iteration; pinning it to `0` (greedy) and adding a seed where 
         -Gemini honours it is the open determinism step (`docs/TODO.md`)
         -, though with a reasoning-capable model bit-exact output isn't 
         -guaranteed anyway, and (1)+(2) already remove the variance that
         - affects the answers. No explicit prompt **caching** yet — the 
         -static system prompt + schema are identical across calls, so im
         -plicit context caching may apply, and cached-token counts are l
         -ogged (`docs/TELEMETRY.md`).                                   
      44 +- **Determinism & reproducibility.** Three levers, in decreasin
         +g order of leverage. **(1) Structured-output mode** — `response
         +_format={"type": "json_object", "response_schema": PatientLabel
         +s.model_json_schema(), "enforce_validation": true}`: a Pydantic
         +-derived schema constrains the *shape* and enum membership, and
         + LiteLLM validates the reply (raising `JSONSchemaValidationErro
         +r` on a mismatch), so format/parse variance is removed and down
         +stream pandas never meets an off-vocabulary value. **(2) Persis
         +ted predictions** — each chunk writes one validated JSON per pa
         +tient to `data/out/`, and the whole analysis (this README) read
         +s *only* those frozen artefacts with no API call, so every numb
         +er and plot is **exactly reproducible run-to-run** regardless o
         +f LLM nondeterminism; this is the real guarantee. **(3) Decodin
         +g params** — `temperature` is **pinned to `0`** (greedy) in `co
         +nfig.json` and imported by `extract.py` — extraction here is a 
         +**classification** task, so we want *same transcript → same lab
         +els*, not sampled variety. This build is the live test case for
         + *why*: a fresh re-run at the provider default shifted long-tai
         +l outputs (the GP-node count; which biologics got mislabelled `
         +before_biologic`) while the headline answers held — exactly the
         + run-to-run drift `0` removes. Temperature is **logged per run*
         +* (with the model) so runs at different parameter levels stay c
         +omparable. A seed would add belt-and-braces, but with a reasoni
         +ng-capable model `0` *minimises* rather than *guarantees* bit-i
         +dentical output; **(2)** is the hard reproducibility guarantee 
         +for the analysis. No explicit prompt **caching** yet — the stat
         +ic system prompt + schema are identical across calls, so implic
         +it context caching may apply, and cached-token counts are logge
         +d (`docs/TELEMETRY.md`).

         -* a false negative in the gold audit — `docs/TO_REVIEW.md`) bec
         -ause truncation is a lexical/structural signal the narrative un
         -der-determines; **`treatment_class`** drifts (un-normalised fre
         -e-text categories inflate the Q3 count); and the **GP node** (`
         -primary_care_contact`) is under-emitted (9/50), weakening the s
         -trict Q4 step count. Fix-first order: a deterministic tail-of-t
         -ext churn rule, a treatment/biologic registry to normalise clas
         -ses, and a larger, multi-annotator gold set — today's is single
         --annotator over 19/50, so the metric carries real sampling unce
         -rtainty.

         +iews_ground_truth_v5.ods`, scored on the `to_review` validation
         + split (19 cases with a non-null label). Headline: `biologic_ta
         +ken` — the field Q1 rests on — scores a high F1 (precision / re
         +call / F1 computed inline in the Q1 gold-eval table below), so 
         +the "% on a biologic" answer is well-supported. Where it's shak
         +y: **`churn`** is unreliable in both directions (a false positi

         +tive under-determines; **`treatment_class`** drifts (un-normali
         +sed free-text categories inflate the Q3 count); and the **GP no
         +de** (`primary_care_contact`) is under-emitted (9/50), weakenin
         +g the strict Q4 step count. Fix-first order: a deterministic ta
         +il-of-text churn rule, a treatment/biologic registry to normali

         + single-annotator over 19/50, so the metric carries real sampli

      190 -> **`unspecified` (Q2) — null handling.** The 2 `unspecified` 
          -patients are biologics **prescribed but not taken** where the 
          -model extracted **no `ReasonNotTaken`** — a genuine null, not 
          -a stated reason. It's relabelled from `(null)` for readability
          - and kept **visible as its own category** rather than dropped 
          -— consistent with the schema's stance on nulls (*Pydantic Sche
          -ma Design → "Three states, not one null"*): absence, negation 
          -and truncation are distinct first-class states, and `UNKNOWN` 
          -/ `NOT_APPLICABLE` exist so a missing value is never silently 
          -conflated with a real one. The fix (prompt the model to always
          - set a reason, default `UNKNOWN`) is tracked in `docs/TODO.md`
          -.

          + is a biologic **prescribed but not taken** where the model ex

          + reason. It's relabelled from `(null)` for readability and kep

      40  **Answers (provisional).**
      41  
      42  - **Single-shot, batched — not multi-stage.** One system prompt
           extracts *all* `PatientLabels` fields in a single structured c
          all per patient; transcripts are then **batched** (10 per reque
          st) only to fit Gemini's free-tier ~20-requests/day cap — batch
          ing ≠ staging, it's still one pass over every field, just sever
          al patients per call. Single-shot minimises calls (decisive und
          er the rate cap), keeps the code simple, and lets the model set
           inter-dependent fields (`biologic_prescribed`/`biologic_taken`
          , `churn`, `referral_pathway`) with the whole story in view. Th
          e cost: every field competes for attention in one prompt, so th
          e hard fields suffer — the EDA shows `churn` unreliable and the
           GP node under-emitted. A multi-stage split (per-section prompt
          s run concurrently, with inspectable intermediates) is the #1 i
          tem in *Next Steps* below; it trades more calls + orchestration
           for better per-field accuracy.
      43 -- **Uncertainty: absence vs. negation vs. truncation.** The pro
         -mpt separates them by construction, onto the three schema mecha
         -nisms above. A global rule — *"Leave a field null or empty when
         - the transcript does not support a confident value"* — covers *
         -*absence**. **Negation** rides on the prescribed-vs-taken boole
         -ans + the `ReasonNotTaken` enum. **Truncation** gets the most e
         -xplicit instruction: judge `churn` from how the transcript *end
         -s*, treat a trailing "…" as a cut-off *only* if "…" isn't a rec
         -urring stylistic device, prefer `false` when ambiguous, and — c
         -ritically — *"keep this truncation signal distinct from a topic
         - simply being absent … (that is 'not mentioned', not churn)."* 
         -Honest limit: truncation is a lexical/structural property of th
         -e transcript's end that the narrative under-determines, so the 
         -instruction alone isn't enough — the gold audit caught a false 
         -positive (P016) and a false negative (P049), and a deterministi
         -c tail-of-text rule would do better (`docs/TO_REVIEW.md`).     
      43 +- **Uncertainty: absence vs. negation vs. truncation.** The pro
         +mpt separates them by construction, onto the three schema mecha
         +nisms above. A global rule — *"Leave a field null or empty when
         + the transcript does not support a confident value"* — covers *
         +*absence**. **Negation** rides on the prescribed-vs-taken boole
         +ans + the `ReasonNotTaken` enum. **Truncation** gets the most e
         +xplicit instruction: judge `churn` from how the transcript *end
         +s*, treat a trailing "…" as a cut-off *only* if "…" isn't a rec
         +urring stylistic device, prefer `false` when ambiguous, and — c
         +ritically — *"keep this truncation signal distinct from a topic
         + simply being absent … (that is 'not mentioned', not churn)."* 
         +Honest limit: truncation is a lexical/structural property of th
         +e transcript's end that the narrative under-determines, so the 
         +instruction alone isn't enough — the gold audit found it unreli
         +able in both directions (over-flagging and missed truncation), 
         +and a deterministic tail-of-text rule would do better (`docs/TO
         +_REVIEW.md`).                                                  
      44  - **Determinism & reproducibility.** Three levers, in decreasin
          g order of leverage. **(1) Structured-output mode** — `response
          _format={"type": "json_object", "response_schema": PatientLabel
          s.model_json_schema(), "enforce_validation": true}`: a Pydantic
          -derived schema constrains the *shape* and enum membership, and
           LiteLLM validates the reply (raising `JSONSchemaValidationErro
          r` on a mismatch), so format/parse variance is removed and down
          stream pandas never meets an off-vocabulary value. **(2) Persis
          ted predictions** — each chunk writes one validated JSON per pa
          tient to `data/out/`, and the whole analysis (this README) read
          s *only* those frozen artefacts with no API call, so every numb
          er and plot is **exactly reproducible run-to-run** regardless o
          f LLM nondeterminism; this is the real guarantee. **(3) Decodin
          g params** — `temperature` is **pinned to `0`** (greedy) in `co
          nfig.json` and imported by `extract.py` — extraction here is a 
          **classification** task, so we want *same transcript → same lab
          els*, not sampled variety. This build is the live test case for
           *why*: a fresh re-run at the provider default shifted long-tai
          l outputs (the GP-node count; which biologics got mislabelled `
          before_biologic`) while the headline answers held — exactly the
           run-to-run drift `0` removes. Temperature is **logged per run*
          * (with the model) so runs at different parameter levels stay c
          omparable. A seed would add belt-and-braces, but with a reasoni
          ng-capable model `0` *minimises* rather than *guarantees* bit-i
          dentical output; **(2)** is the hard reproducibility guarantee 
          for the analysis. No explicit prompt **caching** yet — the stat
          ic system prompt + schema are identical across calls, so implic
          it context caching may apply, and cached-token counts are logge
          d (`docs/TELEMETRY.md`).

      221 -> **Known extraction error (Q3) — error-handling note.** The `
          -biologic` bar conflates two things: genuine **biologic switchi
          -ng** (P043: Remicade → Humira — an earlier biologic legitimate
          -ly tried before the one taken) and a **mislabel** (P046: the *
          -taken* biologic, Humira, is itself flagged `before_biologic = 
          -true`, which can't be right — a treatment isn't "before" itsel
          -f). So "treatments before a biologic" currently also counts bi
          -ologics. We **surface this rather than silently drop it**: a c
          -lear include/exclude rule for biologics, plus the prompt fix f
          -or the P046 mislabel, is tracked in `docs/TODO.md`.           
      221 +> **Known extraction error (Q3) — error-handling note.** A `bi
          +ologic` appearing in "treatments before a biologic" carries `b
          +efore_biologic = true` on a biologic — which is either a genui
          +ne **biologic switch** (an earlier biologic tried before the o
          +ne ultimately taken) or a **model mislabel** (the *taken* biol
          +ogic itself flagged `before_biologic`, which can't be right — 
          +a treatment isn't "before" itself); the per-patient `treatment
          +_records` distinguish the two. So "treatments before a biologi
          +c" currently also counts biologics. We **surface this rather t
          +han silently drop it**: a clear include/exclude rule for biolo
          +gics, plus the prompt fix, is tracked in `docs/TODO.md`.

         +t `temperature` is `1.0`) shifted long-tail outputs (the GP-nod
         +e count; which biologics got mislabelled `before_biologic`) whi
         +le the headline answers held — exactly the run-to-run drift `0`
         + removes. Temperature is **logged per run** (with the model) so
         + runs at different parameter levels stay comparable. A seed wou
         +ld add belt-and-braces, but with a reasoning-capable model `0` 
         +*minimises* rather than *guarantees* bit-identical output; **(2
         +)** is the hard reproducibility guarantee for the analysis. No 
         +explicit prompt **caching** yet — the static system prompt + sc
         +hema are identical across calls, so implicit context caching ma
         +y apply, and cached-token counts are logged (`docs/TELEMETRY.md
         +`).

      161 +def churn_false_negatives(df: pd.DataFrame, gold_path: Path = 
          +GOLD) -> list[str]:

          +"] == 1), "patient_id"]                                       
      166 +    model_churn = dict(zip(df["patient_id"], df["churn"]))    
      167 +    return [pid for pid in flagged if model_churn.get(pid) is 
          +not True]

⏺ Quick answer on the commands, then I'll fix the edit:

  uv run dg dev -m pipeline -d src (yours) vs. dg launch --assets "*" (mine) — 
  same work, different driver:
  - dg dev starts the Dagster dev server (UI at http://127.0.0.1:3000 + daemon),
  long-running. You then click Materialize all in the UI and watch each asset
  (interviews → predictions → {readme, referral_graphs}, docsite) go green/red
  stage-by-stage.
  - dg launch is a one-shot headless run that materializes and exits — no UI.

  Both load the same pipeline.py, so both route through the same loguru sink →
  logs/dagster.log (the one I verified offline). Your way is better for this run
  — you see exactly which stage fails if anything does, and can re-materialize
  a single asset without re-extracting. 👍 Go ahead with dg dev + UI.

⏺ Great question — and the honest answer is mostly a cautionary one, with a real
  signal hiding in the logs rather than the mixed data. Let me pull the token
  telemetry to make it concrete:
  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Here's the real answer — and it turned out better than expected, because the
  logs accidentally ran the exact experiment we'd want.

  First, the cautionary half: the mixed data/out tells us nothing about
  temperature on its own. P001–P010 (temp 0) and P011–P050 (temp 1.0) are
  different patients, so any difference in their aggregate stats is a cohort
  effect, not a temperature effect. You cannot read a temp signal off the mix —
  that's the trap, and it's why we treat the mix as provisional, not as a
  comparison.

  But the extract.log telemetry holds a genuine same-input comparison, because
  the same P001–P010 batch (identical transcripts, identical prompt + schema)
  was extracted five times today:

  ┌───────┬────────────────────────┬─────────────┐
  │  run  │ P001–P010 total_tokens │ temperature │
  ├───────┼────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ 10:50 │                 11,570 │ default 1.0 │
  ├───────┼────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ 12:41 │                 12,260 │ default 1.0 │
  ├───────┼────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ 16:34 │                 12,197 │ default 1.0 │
  ├───────┼────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ 17:14 │                 12,034 │ 0           │
  ├───────┼────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ 17:15 │                 12,034 │ 0           │
  └───────┴────────────────────────┴─────────────┘

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  This is eval option 3 (the "consistency check") realised by accident, and the
  result is textbook:
  - temp 1.0 drifts: same input → 11,570 / 12,260 / 12,197, a ~6% spread. Input
  tokens are fixed, so a varying total means the model emitted different output 
  content each run — run-to-run nondeterminism, exactly what the README warns
  about.
  - temp 0 is reproducible: the two temp-0 runs are byte-identical (12,034 = 
  12,034). Same input → same output. That's the determinism guarantee,
  demonstrated, not asserted.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Honest caveats: total_tokens is a coarse, lower-bound proxy — it only catches
  drift that changes output length; two different extractions of equal length
  would look identical here, so true field-level drift at temp 1.0 is likely
  larger than 6%. And the temp-0 sample is n=2 from one session (greedy decoding
  is the cause, though implicit prompt-caching could reinforce it). The clean
  version of this test is tomorrow: run all 50 twice at each temperature and
  diff the actual JSON fields.

  This is strong enough that I'd suggest folding one line of it into the
  Determinism section — it converts "a re-run shifted long-tail outputs" from a
  claim into a cited measurement (12,034 = 12,034 at temp 0 vs. an 11,570–12,260
  spread at default). Want me to add that? Either way I still need to re-render
  README before we commit (the provisional note I just added is in .qmd but not
  yet in .md).

  output content each run — run-to-run nondeterminism, exactly what the README  
  warns about.                                                                  
  - temp 0 is reproducible: the two temp-0 runs are byte-identical (12,034 =    
  12,034). Same input → same output. That's the determinism guarantee,          
  demonstrated, not asserted.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  The old diagram encoded a false architecture — main.py as the orchestrator, a
  linear in→extract→aggregate→answers chain. The rewrite captures the property
  that actually matters for review: library-first. Dagster, the CLI, and the
  gate are three peers sitting on the same src/ functions — not a hierarchy
  where one shells out to another. That's why the diagram puts them in one
  "Entry points" box and draws the CLI's dotted same src/ funcs edge straight to
  the predictions asset: it shows that dg launch and python src/main.py run
  identical code, which is exactly what makes the standalone CLI a trustworthy
  way to test one stage of the orchestrated pipeline. A diagram that drew them
  as a chain would hide that.

      144 +def pick_cyclic_example(records: list[dict]) -> str:          
      145 +    """A representative patient whose journey *loops* (a phase
          + recurs), chosen deterministically —                          
      146 +    the shortest cyclic pathway, ties broken by lowest id — so
          + the README example renders the same                          
      147 +    run-to-run (no hardcoded id). Falls back to the shortest p
          +athway overall if none cycle."""                              
      148 +    cyclic = [                                                
      149 +        r                                                     
      150 +        for r in records                                      
      151 +        if len(r["referral_pathway"]) != len(set(r["referral_p
          +athway"]))                                                    
      152 +    ]                                                         
      153 +    pool = cyclic or records                                  
      154 +    return min(                                               
      155 +        pool, key=lambda r: (len(r["referral_pathway"]), int(r
          +["patient_id"][1:]))                                          
      156 +    )["patient_id"]                                           
      157 +                                                              
      158 +                                                              
      159 +def pathway_mermaid(records: list[dict], pid: str) -> str:    
      160 +    """A GitHub-renderable Mermaid flowchart of one patient's 
          +``referral_pathway`` — the static                             
      161 +    in-README preview of the interactive pyvis graph. A revisi
          +ted phase is one node the journey                             
      162 +    loops back to; an edge's ``xN`` label is its traversal cou
          +nt."""                                                        
      163 +    seq = next(r["referral_pathway"] for r in records if r["pa
          +tient_id"] == pid)                                            
      164 +    ids = {step: f"n{i}" for i, step in enumerate(dict.fromkey
          +s(seq))}                                                      
      165 +    edges = Counter(zip(seq, seq[1:]))                        
      166 +    lines = ["```mermaid", "flowchart LR"]                    
      167 +    lines += [f'    {nid}["{step}"]' for step, nid in ids.item
          +s()]                                                          
      168 +    lines += [                                                
      169 +        f"    {ids[a]} -->{f'|x{c}| ' if c > 1 else ''}{ids[b]
          +}"                                                            
      170 +        for (a, b), c in edges.items()                        
      171 +    ]                                                         
      172 +    lines.append("```")                                       
      173 +    return "\n".join(lines)                                   
      174 +                                                              
      175 +

❯ the SYSTEM_DESIGN.md rendered fine so mirror that syntax for Mermaid 

⏺ Confirmed identical — no change needed. Both the working SYSTEM_DESIGN.md
  (line 18) and my README teaser (line 366) emit the exact same fence: ``` 
  mermaid. Since SYSTEM_DESIGN.md renders fine on GitHub, the teaser will too —
  they're byte-for-byte the same syntax.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  My earlier "count 0" was a false alarm from grepping the no-space variant
  (```mermaid). Quarto/pandoc's GFM writer always emits ``` mermaid with a space
  — and per CommonMark the info string is whitespace-trimmed, so GitHub reads
  the language as mermaid either way and renders the diagram. The lesson: the
  dynamic print()-via-output: asis path produces the same GFM as a native
  {mermaid} cell, so generating the diagram from data at render time (no
  hardcoded pathway) costs nothing in compatibility.

⏺ That's expected GitHub behaviour, not a bug — and it's worth being precise
  about, because it affects more than this one file.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  GitHub's repo (blob) view never renders committed .html as a web page — it
  always shows the source, for security (arbitrary HTML/JS in a repo view would
  be an XSS vector). This hits all 51 files the same way: index.html and every
  referral_pathway_P*.html. They render only when (a) opened from a local clone
  in a browser, or (b) served by GitHub Pages / a proxy like
  htmlpreview.github.io — and both of those need the repo public (your repo is
  private, which we verified in the security check), or GitHub Pro for private
  Pages. This is exactly why I made the Mermaid teaser the in-README preview:
  Mermaid renders natively in GitHub's Markdown view, so it's the one pathway
  visualisation a reviewer sees without cloning.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  So the question is how you want a reviewer to reach the interactive graphs.
  The choice changes what I write in the README:

❯ can quarto be used for the rendering? 

⏺ You chose local viewing, keep private — noted. And yes, you're right: the
  per-case graphs are source-only on GitHub too, interactive only locally.

  To your question — yes, Quarto is actually the clean fix here, via Mermaid.
  Quarto rendering pyvis HTML wouldn't help (HTML is HTML — GitHub still shows
  source). But Quarto can render every pathway as a Mermaid diagram into a
  Markdown gallery (docs/referral_pathways.md), and Mermaid does render natively
  in GitHub's Markdown view — no clone, no Pages, private-repo-fine. We already
  have the pathway_mermaid() function; I'd just loop it over all 50.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  This gives you two complementary artefacts from the same pathway_mermaid()
  code: the pyvis HTML (rich, interactive — hover/drag/zoom — but local-only)
  and a Quarto→Mermaid Markdown gallery (static, but renders for anyone on
  GitHub). The pyvis version trades portability for interactivity; the Mermaid
  version trades interactivity for portability. Building both from one function
  is cheap and lets the reviewer pick: skim all 50 on GitHub, or clone for the
  interactive deep-dive.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  This is the payoff of having pathway_mermaid() as a tested src/ function
  rather than inline qmd: the same function now feeds three surfaces — the
  README teaser (1 pathway), the full GitHub-viewable gallery (50 pathways), and
  the unit test — and the one-line theme change (%%{init: 
  {'theme':'neutral'}}%%) propagated to all of them at once. The neutral theme
  forces a light background via Mermaid's init directive, which GitHub honours
  even in dark mode, so the diagrams read the same for every reviewer regardless
  of their colour-mode. You now have the full trade-off covered:
  static-but-portable (Mermaid, renders anywhere) and interactive-but-local
  (pyvis, data/out/html/).

❯ remind me how to build the mkdocs docsite locally to test                     

⏺ Here's the local docsite workflow — use serve for testing (live,
  hot-reloading):

  uv run mkdocs serve     # live preview at http://127.0.0.1:8000 — hot-reloads 
  on every save
  uv run mkdocs build     # one-off static render to site/ (git-ignored), what 
  the Dagster docsite asset runs

  To verify the gallery specifically: with serve running, open
  http://127.0.0.1:8000, click "Referral pathways" in the left nav, and you
  should see the 50 journey diagrams rendered (light/neutral theme) rather than
  code blocks. Stop the server with Ctrl-C.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  serve vs build: serve runs an in-memory dev server that re-renders on file
  change (ideal for iterating on referral_pathways.qmd → re-render to .md →
  watch it update); build writes the static site/ once. One caveat now that we
  use mkdocs-mermaid2-plugin: it injects mermaid@10.4.0 from a CDN, so the
  browser needs internet on first load for the diagrams to render — the build is
  offline-fine, but the rendering (client-side) isn't. If you ever need
  fully-offline rendering, the plugin can be pointed at a vendored
  mermaid.min.js via extra_javascript instead.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Since mkdocs serve is a long-running server, run it in your own terminal (or
  prefix with ! in this session if you want its output here, though you'll need
  to Ctrl-C to free the session). Everything's still staged-and-ready from the
  last step — /export then commit/push whenever you're set.
